     1	# E4: can the model be its own oracle? (one mind, two channels)
     2	
     3	Predictions and the measurement protocol below were frozen before any oracle call. The frozen text is not edited. Later sections add the script's output.
     4	
     5	Population: the 60 class-(a) problems from E3 (clock-mode held-out R1 and R2, seed 777), each with the first 16 words of the written-built W-method suite at bound n+2, and a control of 60 problems whose written program is equivalent to the reference, matched to the same template counts.
     6	
     7	## Predictions (frozen)
     8	
     9	1. **Many class-(a) suites cannot be asked.** The suite is built from the written machine. On the coin problems and the adder, that alphabet is not the question's event vocabulary (bigrams, bare digits, digit pairs), so those words are skipped. A perfect word-oracle still cannot catch a program whose distinguishing words are not phrases of the question. The catch rate on all 60 therefore sits well below E3's 57/60 exposure figure. The binding limit is the words that cannot be rendered, not the length of the suite.
    10	
    11	2. **Where the alphabet is the question's own, one event is enough to catch a bad table.** Parking, the drone, the robot and the shelf use the question's own event words, and E3's distinguishing words are short. O1, answering in words, will get most of those one-step questions right even though it compiled a wrong table. I expect O1 to catch most of that slice and almost none of the coin slice. Overall, O1's catch rate lands around a third to a half of the 60. The strict shared-misreading rate — every one of 16 words rendered, and every answer equal to the wrong program — stays under about one in five, because the coin suites are not fully renderable and because a right one-step answer disagrees with the bad program.
    12	
    13	3. **A fixed paraphrase will not decorrelate the reading.** O2's three rewrites keep every number and every event symbol. O1∧O2's catch will sit close to O1's. Where O1 follows the wrong program, O2 usually follows it too.
    14	
    15	4. **The base model is a second channel, not a free catch.** O3 was not trained to emit these tables, so on the renderable slice it should catch at least as often as O1. The original log is still in the prompt, and a 4k thinking cap will cut some traces off before an answer. I expect O3's catch near O1's, O1∨O3 only a little higher, and O3's false-alarm rate higher than O1's.
    16	
    17	5. **False alarms.** On a correct program the short case is computable from the question. I expect O1's false-alarm rate under about one in five. O3's higher, up to about two in five, from answering the original log or stopping inside the think block.
    18	
    19	6. **Correlation.** On problems where the separating word was actually asked, a miss on that word will move with the error rate on the other short words. I expect a positive correlation: a misread rule fails many short cases, and a correct reading fails few. A correlation near zero would mean the model is right in general and wrong only on the program's particular bug. I do not expect that to be the main pattern. The result that matters either way is the floor from words the question cannot say.
    20	
    21	## Measurement protocol (frozen)
    22	
    23	Scripts: `research/theory/tests/e4/`. The item file is built by `build_items.py` from E3's machine code. Oracle text is produced by `e4_oracle.py` on Modal. Rates are written by `score.py`. Key counts are computed a second way in the same run; the run stops if the two ways disagree.
    24	
    25	### Items
    26	
    27	Class (a) is E3's 60, in E3's walk order. The machine is the first inequivalent judged call, as in E3. The suite is the written machine, minimised, bound m = (reachable states of the written machine) + 2, words in length-then-lex order, the first 16 (or the whole suite if it is shorter). Exposure of those words must match E3's stored `written|m_written_plus` `exposed16` flag, or the build stops.
    28	
    29	Controls: for each template, the same count as class (a), taking the earliest problem that is not class (a) and whose first total judged machine is equivalent to that slot's reference. A control whose suite separates the two machines is rejected. The answer formula is checked on the original reference tape against the stored answer, for every class-(a) problem and every control. A mismatch stops the build.
    30	
    31	### Rendering
    32	
    33	A symbol renders only as an event phrase of the question.
    34	
    35	- If the reference tape appears as a contiguous list (separator `, `, ` `, or `,`), each listed symbol's phrase is itself, and the same separator is used.
    36	- Otherwise, for a rumor chain, the claims align with `is lying` / `is honest` after the opening sentence. The phrase map has to reproduce the reference tape, or the build stops.
    37	- A Mealy digit pair is not an event phrase. Those words are skipped.
    38	- The empty word renders as `none`.
    39	- A word containing any symbol without a phrase is skipped and counted. It is not an answer.
    40	
    41	The oracle sentence, appended after the question, is: "Using exactly the same rules, suppose the events were <rendered>. What would the answer be? Answer in words; do not write a program."
    42	
    43	### What is compared
    44	
    45	The answer is the quantity the question asks for, not the raw state trace. The formula is the template's own: parking and shelf fees from the final count and the price in the question; drone distance back to the start; robot distance to the charger named in the question; rumor payoff from the final honest/liar bit; coin sum or difference from the final even/odd bit and the two amounts in the question. The reference answer uses the reference machine's final state. The program answer uses the written machine's final state and the same formula. If the written state is not in the formula's domain, the program answer is missing: that case cannot catch and cannot count as agreement.
    46	
    47	A parsed oracle answer matches when the numbers compare as in REASON (last number, leading zeros dropped) or, for yes/no, the last yes or no. O3 is parsed only after `</think>`; an unclosed think is no answer. O1 and O2 are parsed on the text before `<program`. An unparsed reply is a miss for per-case accuracy. It is not a catch, not agreement with the program, and not a false alarm.
    48	
    49	### Oracles
    50	
    51	- **O1.** Merged `reason-s1/adapter_step500`, merged fresh in the job. Deployed non-thinking chat template, greedy (temperature 0), at most 512 new tokens, no program execution.
    52	- **O2.** The same model and sampling. The question is reworded by one of three fixed templates, chosen by the problem's ordinal modulo 3: a specification frame; the final question sentence moved to the front; or the substitutions "What is the"/"What are the" → "Report the", "How many" → "Count the", "How much" → "Compute the", "Give the number" → "Report the number". No language model writes the paraphrase.
    53	- **O3.** Base Qwen3-4B, no adapter. Thinking template, temperature 0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, seed the first 8 hex digits of sha256 of `case_id + ":" + oracle`, at most 4096 new tokens.
    54	
    55	### Rates
    56	
    57	Over the 60 class-(a) problems, and over the 60 controls:
    58	
    59	- **Catch:** at least one rendered answer matches the reference and differs from the program.
    60	- **Shared misreading:** the suite has 16 words, every one of them was rendered, and every answer equals the program.
    61	- **False alarm:** at least one rendered control answer differs from the correct program.
    62	- **Per-case accuracy:** rendered, generated cases whose parsed answer equals the reference.
    63	- **O1∧O2 / O1∨O3:** the same catch and false-alarm events, intersected or unioned at the problem.
    64	
    65	Wilson 95% intervals on those rates.
    66	
    67	**Correlation.** For each class-(a) problem, the separating case is the first of the 16 suite words on which the two machines' outputs differ. If that word was not rendered, the problem is excluded. X is 1 when the oracle misses the reference on that word. Y is the oracle's error rate on the other rendered words. Report Pearson r, and the mean of Y when X is 1 and when X is 0.
    68	
    69	### Run
    70	
    71	Modal app `polyformer-e4`, volume `polyformer-e4-results`. The image hashes `e4_oracle.py` and `e4_modal.py` and refuses a mismatch. The item file's sha256 is checked before generation. Ledger entries are reserved before launch, with the GPU named at reservation and the worker's GPU and CPU recorded from the host. Cap $15, upper-bound rate (GPU plus 16 CPU and 64 GiB). Smoke is the first two rendered words of the first two class-(a) problems that have two renderable words, on all three oracles. Those outputs are read before the full run. Jobs are spawned and polled; nothing long-lived is waited on in the foreground.
    72

   292	4 E5
   293	4 X3
   294	5 E6
   295	5 X4
   296	6 E7
   297	6 X5
   298	7 E8
   299	7 X6
   300	8 E8
   301	8 X7
   302	answer: The lot ends with {n} cars.
   303	</program>
   304	<exec/> The lot ends with 8 cars. At the end pay 3 × 8 = 24 dollars.
   305	
   306	Answer: Twenty-four dollars.
   307	```
   308	
   309	## What could be wrong
   310	
   311	The empty word is rendered as the phrase none. A Mealy word, or any word whose symbol is not an event phrase of the question, is skipped and is not an answer. Skipping keeps a wrong alphabet from being asked in words the question never used, and it also means those problems cannot be caught or counted as shared misreadings. Rumor phrases are the question's own is lying and is honest; a written symbol outside that map is skipped, and a written state that is not honest or liar does not yield a program answer.
   312	
   313	Class (a) vocabularies: {"list": 53, "none": 3, "gloss": 4}. Skip reasons, class (a): {"unrendered": 442}; controls: {"unrendered": 16}.
   314	
   315	Generation diagnostics: O1:hit_cap 780, O1:prog_unreadable 16, O1:scored 1461, O1:unparsed 479, O1:wrote_program 1461, O2:hit_cap 597, O2:prog_unreadable 16, O2:scored 1461, O2:unparsed 588, O2:wrote_program 1461, O3:hit_cap 1154, O3:prog_unreadable 16, O3:scored 1461, O3:unparsed 1218.
   316	
   317	Answer formulas were checked on the original tape of every class-(a) problem and every control before any oracle call; a mismatch would have stopped the item build. Exposure of the first 16 written-suite words matched E3's stored exposed16 flag on all 60 class-(a) problems. Control suites did not separate an equivalent program from the reference. O3 with an unclosed think is scored as no answer. A reply that states no parseable answer does not count as agreeing or as a false alarm; it counts as a miss for per-case accuracy.
   318	
   319	The smoke replies opened with a program and stated the fee afterwards, so the span before the first program was empty. The rates above read the last line that begins with Answer in the prose outside program blocks. A program reply with no such line is unparsed. A number written as words is read when that line has no digits. This reading was fixed after the smoke and before the full run.
   320	
   321	Full-run host: `{  "gpu": "NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3",  "cpu": "GenuineIntel | 6 | 143 | unknown | avx512f=True" }`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# E3: would "short teaches long" have caught Savant's mistakes?
     2	
     3	Predictions and the measurement protocol below were frozen before any equivalence check, suite, or class count was computed. The frozen text is not edited. Later sections add the script's output.
     4	
     5	Population, frozen here: the counted clock-mode held-out runs, R1 (`r1a-clock`, `r1b-clock`) and R2 (`r2-clock`), seed 777. One class per wrong problem. Correct problems are used only to check that a suite built from an equivalent written table agrees with the reference.
     6	
     7	## Predictions (frozen)
     8	
     9	1. **Class (a) is real and smaller than the failures.** It will sit on the templates where §7's root cause is the table (coin, parking, drone), and it will be a thin slice of R2. Minimising both machines will not turn those table failures into matches: the recorded mistakes are wrong alphabets and wrong rows, not renamings of the same map. Some of §7's table column will leave (a) and land in (d), because a Moore header on Mealy rows is not a machine the W-method can talk about.
    10	
    11	2. **Class (b) is the bulk of what a characteristic suite cannot touch.** Quote and notes failures keep the right map and feed it the wrong string. That is most of R2 and a large part of R1. Class (c) is the small residue: the call's machine and its tape match the reference, and the English after the final is wrong (a fee, a distance, a count of fixed points). Class (d) absorbs cut-off transcripts, missing calls, citation lines with no table, and programs that are not single-valued total functions.
    12	
    13	3. **Distinguishing words are short.** For a wrong alphabet the machines differ on a single symbol. For a wrong counter or grid row they differ at the access word of that row. The length histogram should pile up at the shortest lengths, not out near the tape length. Length zero is allowed when the start state's output already differs.
    14	
    15	4. **Suite size splits by alphabet.** Under m equal to the reference's reachable states, parity and small counters should often fit in a few dozen cases; a drone product or a digit-pair adder will often exceed a 64-case card. The bound m = written reachable states + 2 multiplies the suite by the sum of |Σ|^i up to that slack, and it will push many suites past 64, especially when Σ is digit pairs. When the written machine has more reachable states than the reference, the reference-sized bound does not certify.
    16	
    17	5. **A cap of 64 shortest cases catches the gross compile errors; a cap of 16 misses some.** Built from the reference, the shortest tests include the short access strings, so most inequivalent total machines should fail inside 64 and fewer inside 16. Built from the written table alone (the deployable suite: no reference at test time), a wrong alphabet is still exposed, because the reference has no output on those symbols. Coverage at 16 and at 64 should be weaker than the reference-built suite whenever the written state count inflates the bound or the bad row sits behind a long access word.
    18	
    19	6. **Correct answers.** The suite built from the written table of a call that is equivalent to the reference will agree with the reference on every case the script executes. A disagreement is a bug in the script, not a property of the runs.
    20	
    21	7. **Headline.** The share of failures that short-teaches-long can address at all is the class (a) share. I expect it well under half of R1 and a small fraction of R2. Among those it can address, the deployable suite (written table, m = written states + 2), cut at 64 shortest cases, should expose most of the total-machine errors and almost none of the quote, notes, citation, and length failures, because those are not in (a).
    22	
    23	## Measurement protocol (frozen)
    24	
    25	Scripts live in `research/theory/tests/e3/`. They are the only source of numbers in the results section. Key counts are computed a second way in the same run and the run stops if the two ways disagree.
    26	
    27	### References
    28	
    29	`ref_calls` on disk in the problems JSON are the references used for the classes. Separately, rebuild 10 problems with `reason_data.example` and the on-disk Qwen3-4B tokenizer, from seed 777 and the cell specs in `jobs/cells.py` / `jobs/gates1.json`, at these positions: R1 indices 0, 17, 64, 100, 128, 200, 300, 383 and R2 indices 0, 80, in cell order as `make_problems` builds them. A rebuilt reference must match the stored tables, tape or numbers, answer, and truth values. A mismatch stops the run.
    30	
    31	### Written text
    32	
    33	Each call's `program_head` is the program text. If it contains no closing program tag, extend it by overlap with `response_tail`: the overlap must be at least 80 characters, and every occurrence of that suffix in the tail must continue identically through `</program>`. Otherwise keep the head and mark the call truncated.
    34	
    35	### Parsing
    36	
    37	Tokenise with the local Qwen3-4B byte-level BPE (vocab and merges on disk, NFC, the Qwen pretokeniser, special tokens longest-first). Parse with `reason_core.parse_call`. Labels are the decoded tokens with surrounding whitespace stripped, so a leading-space name token and the reference's bare name meet.
    38	
    39	A register is a function only when its rows are single-valued. Moore rows are (state, event) → next. Mealy rows are (state, digit, digit) → (next, output). The clock's stored error decides whether that function was accepted:
    40	
    41	- a parse error means the clock did not accept a total machine;
    42	- a reading error, or a call that ran, means the table was total and single-valued.
    43	
    44	`compile` true means the running table was syntactically the reference. That call's machine is the reference machine, even if the saved text is truncated.
    45	
    46	### Machines and equivalence
    47	
    48	- Tape call: one Moore machine. The state is the tuple of register states, the output is that tuple of labels, including the output of the start state on the empty word. Align registers by name when the two name sets are equal, otherwise by order.
    49	- Number call: one Mealy machine. The reference is `table_rows` of the recorded op, start state `0`. Output is the digit sequence only; carry names are hidden. The written machine is its Mealy register. A Moore table against a number reference is a different transducer.
    50	- Reachable states only. Minimisation is partition refinement.
    51	- Two machines are equivalent when every word has the same output. A symbol outside a machine's alphabet makes that run undefined. Undefined against a defined output is a difference.
    52	- Method A for the shortest difference: BFS on the product. Method B: a second search (iterative check of every shorter word when the ball is at most 100000 strings, otherwise an independent BFS). The two lengths must agree.
    53	- Equivalence itself is recomputed by canonical minimisation: minimise, name each class by its output and the access order of the class, and compare the resulting tables. A disagreement with the product stops the run.
    54	
    55	### One class per wrong problem
    56	
    57	Walk calls in order. Judge every successful call before the first error other than "too many calls", and judge that first error call too. Successful calls consume reference slots in order.
    58	
    59	For a judged call:
    60	
    61	- no single-valued table, a parse error, a truncated table whose visible rows do not decide the question, or a missing slot with no accepted machine: **unresolved**;
    62	- an accepted total machine that is not equivalent to its reference slot: **inequivalent**;
    63	- equivalent, but the call's reading is not the reference tape or numbers (a reading error, or `reading_exact` false): **bad input**;
    64	- equivalent and the reading matches: **good**.
    65	
    66	The problem's class, first match:
    67	
    68	- **(a)** if any judged slot is inequivalent;
    69	- **(d)** if any judged slot is unresolved, or a reference slot was never reached;
    70	- **(b)** if some judged slot has a bad input and every judged slot is equivalent or bad input;
    71	- **(c)** if every reference slot is good and the problem is still wrong.
    72	
    73	Class (a) is the only share short-teaches-long can address. The machine measured for a class (a) problem is the first inequivalent judged slot.
    74	
    75	### Suites
    76	
    77	Chow's W-method. The spec is minimised first; n is its minimal state count. S is one shortest access word per state, including the empty word. P is the set S ∪ S·Σ. W is a characterisation set built greedily: state pairs in sorted order, add a shortest distinguishing word when none already in W separates that pair. The empty word is allowed for Moore machines.
    78	
    79	Two bounds, each applied to a spec built from the reference and again to a spec built from the written table:
    80	
    81	- m = the reference's reachable state count (before merging equivalents);
    82	- m = the written machine's reachable state count + 2.
    83	
    84	If m < n the suite does not certify: no size, not exposed. If m ≥ n, k = m − n and the suite is the set of words p·z·w with |z| ≤ k. Size is the cardinality of that set. Words are ordered by length, then lexicographically. Exposed at 16 (at 64) means some word among the first 16 (64) in that order makes the two machines disagree.
    85	
    86	A partial or unparsed table has no suite and is not exposed. It is also not class (a); class (a) is only an accepted total machine that is inequivalent.
    87	
    88	### Correct answers
    89	
    90	For each successful call of a correct problem, build the written machine and compare it to the aligned reference. When the two are equivalent, build the written-table suite at both bounds. If the suite has at most 20000 words, run every word. If it is larger, run every word in the k = 0 slice and every word that falls in the shortest 64, and record that the tail was not executed. Any disagreement on an executed word is a bug and stops the run. A correct problem whose running table is not equivalent is reported; it is a finding, not a suite bug.
    91	
    92	### Second recount
    93	
    94	The script writes one record per problem. Class totals are counted by a direct counter and again by summing per-template counts. Exposure numerators are counted from the boolean on each class (a) record and again by a separate pass over the saved list. Both pairs must match, and every problem's two equivalence methods must match.
    95	
    96	### What the results section must contain
    97	
    98	The four-class table for R1 and for R2, side by side with `reason_anatomy.anatomy` on the same records (the source of §7). The distinguishing-length histogram for class (a). Suite sizes and coverage at 16 and at 64, for both bounds and both origins (reference-built and written-built). The class (a) share of all failures. The correct-answer suite check. A "What could be wrong" note fed by the run's own diagnostics (truncation, stitch checks against saved transcripts, slots the text could not decide, bounds with m < n, recount status).
    99	
   100	## Amendment (before any statistic)
   101	
   102	The class rule above says (d) when a reference slot was never reached. Read literally, a three-call problem whose first call is a right machine and a wrong quote would be (d), because the later calls were never started. That is the quote failure the classes exist to count, not an unparsed transcript.
   103	
   104	Resolved before computing:
   105	
   106	- A slot is judged when a call is paired with it: each successful call, and the first error other than "too many calls".
   107	- Slots after that stopping call are not a second failure.
   108	- (d) for a missing slot applies only when every judged call is good and some reference slot was never judged. The transcript stopped after a correct prefix, or it never called.
   109	- (b) applies when every judged call is an equivalent machine and at least one has a bad input, whether or not later slots were judged.
   110	- (a) still wins when any judged call is inequivalent.

   239	
   240	Calls whose program text was closed in the head: 348; stitched from the response tail: 239; left truncated: 76; rejected because the join did not match the recorded token length: 21; ambiguous overlap: 0.

   243	Class (a) suites with m below the minimised spec: 13. Suites whose exact size was above the enumeration cap: 5.
   244	On a total register, a symbol that register never names is a self-loop, matching the clock. A missing row on a symbol the register does use is not invented. Mealy equivalence ignores carry names and compares output digits. Moore equivalence compares the whole state-label trace, including the start state. A program the clock rejected as not total is class (d), so a Moore header on Mealy rows is outside the certificate. The decisive call is the first real error, not a later repair. Parse errors and truncated tables are (d), including truncated tables whose saved rows already disagree; some of the unresolved calls may still be table failures the text does not show.

     1	# E4: can the model be its own oracle? (one mind, two channels)
     2	
     3	Predictions and the measurement protocol below were frozen before any oracle call. The frozen text is not edited. Later sections add the script's output.
     4	
     5	Population: the 60 class-(a) problems from E3 (clock-mode held-out R1 and R2, seed 777), each with the first 16 words of the written-built W-method suite at bound n+2, and a control of 60 problems whose written program is equivalent to the reference, matched to the same template counts.
     6	
     7	## Predictions (frozen)
     8	
     9	1. **Many class-(a) suites cannot be asked.** The suite is built from the written machine. On the coin problems and the adder, that alphabet is not the question's event vocabulary (bigrams, bare digits, digit pairs), so those words are skipped. A perfect word-oracle still cannot catch a program whose distinguishing words are not phrases of the question. The catch rate on all 60 therefore sits well below E3's 57/60 exposure figure. The binding limit is the words that cannot be rendered, not the length of the suite.
    10	
    11	2. **Where the alphabet is the question's own, one event is enough to catch a bad table.** Parking, the drone, the robot and the shelf use the question's own event words, and E3's distinguishing words are short. O1, answering in words, will get most of those one-step questions right even though it compiled a wrong table. I expect O1 to catch most of that slice and almost none of the coin slice. Overall, O1's catch rate lands around a third to a half of the 60. The strict shared-misreading rate — every one of 16 words rendered, and every answer equal to the wrong program — stays under about one in five, because the coin suites are not fully renderable and because a right one-step answer disagrees with the bad program.
    12	
    13	3. **A fixed paraphrase will not decorrelate the reading.** O2's three rewrites keep every number and every event symbol. O1∧O2's catch will sit close to O1's. Where O1 follows the wrong program, O2 usually follows it too.
    14	
    15	4. **The base model is a second channel, not a free catch.** O3 was not trained to emit these tables, so on the renderable slice it should catch at least as often as O1. The original log is still in the prompt, and a 4k thinking cap will cut some traces off before an answer. I expect O3's catch near O1's, O1∨O3 only a little higher, and O3's false-alarm rate higher than O1's.
    16	
    17	5. **False alarms.** On a correct program the short case is computable from the question. I expect O1's false-alarm rate under about one in five. O3's higher, up to about two in five, from answering the original log or stopping inside the think block.
    18	
    19	6. **Correlation.** On problems where the separating word was actually asked, a miss on that word will move with the error rate on the other short words. I expect a positive correlation: a misread rule fails many short cases, and a correct reading fails few. A correlation near zero would mean the model is right in general and wrong only on the program's particular bug. I do not expect that to be the main pattern. The result that matters either way is the floor from words the question cannot say.
    20	
    21	## Measurement protocol (frozen)
    22	
    23	Scripts: `research/theory/tests/e4/`. The item file is built by `build_items.py` from E3's machine code. Oracle text is produced by `e4_oracle.py` on Modal. Rates are written by `score.py`. Key counts are computed a second way in the same run; the run stops if the two ways disagree.
    24	
    25	### Items
    26	
    27	Class (a) is E3's 60, in E3's walk order. The machine is the first inequivalent judged call, as in E3. The suite is the written machine, minimised, bound m = (reachable states of the written machine) + 2, words in length-then-lex order, the first 16 (or the whole suite if it is shorter). Exposure of those words must match E3's stored `written|m_written_plus` `exposed16` flag, or the build stops.
    28	
    29	Controls: for each template, the same count as class (a), taking the earliest problem that is not class (a) and whose first total judged machine is equivalent to that slot's reference. A control whose suite separates the two machines is rejected. The answer formula is checked on the original reference tape against the stored answer, for every class-(a) problem and every control. A mismatch stops the build.
    30	
    31	### Rendering
    32	
    33	A symbol renders only as an event phrase of the question.
    34	
    35	- If the reference tape appears as a contiguous list (separator `, `, ` `, or `,`), each listed symbol's phrase is itself, and the same separator is used.
    36	- Otherwise, for a rumor chain, the claims align with `is lying` / `is honest` after the opening sentence. The phrase map has to reproduce the reference tape, or the build stops.
    37	- A Mealy digit pair is not an event phrase. Those words are skipped.
    38	- The empty word renders as `none`.
    39	- A word containing any symbol without a phrase is skipped and counted. It is not an answer.
    40	
    41	The oracle sentence, appended after the question, is: "Using exactly the same rules, suppose the events were <rendered>. What would the answer be? Answer in words; do not write a program."
    42	
    43	### What is compared
    44	
    45	The answer is the quantity the question asks for, not the raw state trace. The formula is the template's own: parking and shelf fees from the final count and the price in the question; drone distance back to the start; robot distance to the charger named in the question; rumor payoff from the final honest/liar bit; coin sum or difference from the final even/odd bit and the two amounts in the question. The reference answer uses the reference machine's final state. The program answer uses the written machine's final state and the same formula. If the written state is not in the formula's domain, the program answer is missing: that case cannot catch and cannot count as agreement.
    46	
    47	A parsed oracle answer matches when the numbers compare as in REASON (last number, leading zeros dropped) or, for yes/no, the last yes or no. O3 is parsed only after `</think>`; an unclosed think is no answer. O1 and O2 are parsed on the text before `<program`. An unparsed reply is a miss for per-case accuracy. It is not a catch, not agreement with the program, and not a false alarm.
    48	
    49	### Oracles
    50	
    51	- **O1.** Merged `reason-s1/adapter_step500`, merged fresh in the job. Deployed non-thinking chat template, greedy (temperature 0), at most 512 new tokens, no program execution.
    52	- **O2.** The same model and sampling. The question is reworded by one of three fixed templates, chosen by the problem's ordinal modulo 3: a specification frame; the final question sentence moved to the front; or the substitutions "What is the"/"What are the" → "Report the", "How many" → "Count the", "How much" → "Compute the", "Give the number" → "Report the number". No language model writes the paraphrase.
    53	- **O3.** Base Qwen3-4B, no adapter. Thinking template, temperature 0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, seed the first 8 hex digits of sha256 of `case_id + ":" + oracle`, at most 4096 new tokens.
    54	
    55	### Rates
    56	
    57	Over the 60 class-(a) problems, and over the 60 controls:
    58	
    59	- **Catch:** at least one rendered answer matches the reference and differs from the program.
    60	- **Shared misreading:** the suite has 16 words, every one of them was rendered, and every answer equals the program.
    61	- **False alarm:** at least one rendered control answer differs from the correct program.
    62	- **Per-case accuracy:** rendered, generated cases whose parsed answer equals the reference.
    63	- **O1∧O2 / O1∨O3:** the same catch and false-alarm events, intersected or unioned at the problem.
    64	
    65	Wilson 95% intervals on those rates.
    66	
    67	**Correlation.** For each class-(a) problem, the separating case is the first of the 16 suite words on which the two machines' outputs differ. If that word was not rendered, the problem is excluded. X is 1 when the oracle misses the reference on that word. Y is the oracle's error rate on the other rendered words. Report Pearson r, and the mean of Y when X is 1 and when X is 0.
    68	
    69	### Run
    70	
    71	Modal app `polyformer-e4`, volume `polyformer-e4-results`. The image hashes `e4_oracle.py` and `e4_modal.py` and refuses a mismatch. The item file's sha256 is checked before generation. Ledger entries are reserved before launch, with the GPU named at reservation and the worker's GPU and CPU recorded from the host. Cap $15, upper-bound rate (GPU plus 16 CPU and 64 GiB). Smoke is the first two rendered words of the first two class-(a) problems that have two renderable words, on all three oracles. Those outputs are read before the full run. Jobs are spawned and polled; nothing long-lived is waited on in the foreground.
    72	
    73	<!-- BEGIN RESULTS -->
    74	## Results
    75	
    76	Computed by `research/theory/tests/e4/score.py` from the item file and the full-run transcripts.
    77	Rates are Wilson 95% intervals. Catch, shared misreading and false alarm are over the 60 problems;
    78	a problem whose words were skipped is not a catch, not a shared misreading and not a false alarm.
    79	Per-case accuracy is over rendered cases that were generated. Two counting passes agreed.

     1	"""Moore and Mealy machines, equivalence, and Chow's W-method.
     2	
     3	Two equivalence checks are independent: a product search, and canonical minimisation.
     4	A suite failure on a pair the two checks call equivalent is a bug.
     5	"""
     6	
     7	from __future__ import annotations
     8	
     9	import heapq
    10	from collections import deque
    11	from itertools import product
    12	
    13	
    14	def _sym_key(a):
    15	    if isinstance(a, tuple):
    16	        return (1,) + tuple(str(x) for x in a)
    17	    return (0, str(a))
    18	
    19	
    20	def word_key(word):
    21	    return (len(word), tuple(_sym_key(a) for a in word))
    22	
    23	
    24	class Machine:
    25	    def __init__(self, kind, alphabet, start, delta, out):
    26	        self.kind = kind
    27	        self.alphabet = tuple(sorted(alphabet, key=_sym_key))
    28	        self.start = start
    29	        self.delta = delta
    30	        self.out = out
    31	        self.states = tuple(_reachable(start, self.alphabet, delta))
    32	
    33	    def total(self):
    34	        return all((s, a) in self.delta for s in self.states for a in self.alphabet)
    35	
    36	
    37	def _reachable(start, alphabet, delta):
    38	    seen = []
    39	    have = set()
    40	    q = deque([start])
    41	    have.add(start)
    42	    while q:
    43	        s = q.popleft()
    44	        seen.append(s)
    45	        for a in alphabet:
    46	            n = delta.get((s, a))
    47	            if n is not None and n not in have:
    48	                have.add(n)
    49	                q.append(n)
    50	    return seen
    51	
    52	
    53	def moore(alphabet, start, delta):
    54	    """`delta` maps (state, symbol) to the next state. The output of a state is the state."""
    55	    return Machine("moore", alphabet, start, delta, {s: s for s, _ in delta.keys()} | {start: start})
    56	
    57	
    58	def mealy(alphabet, start, delta, out):
    59	    """`delta` maps (state, symbol) to the next state. `out` maps (state, symbol) to the output."""
    60	    return Machine("mealy", alphabet, start, delta, out)
    61	
    62	
    63	def run_from(m, state, word):
    64	    """('ok', outputs) or ('undef', outputs_so_far, symbol)."""
    65	    s = state
    66	    if m.kind == "moore":
    67	        outs = [m.out[s]]
    68	        for a in word:
    69	            n = m.delta.get((s, a))
    70	            if n is None:
    71	                return ("undef", tuple(outs), a)
    72	            s = n
    73	            outs.append(m.out[s])
    74	        return ("ok", tuple(outs))
    75	    outs = []
    76	    for a in word:
    77	        n = m.delta.get((s, a))
    78	        if n is None:
    79	            return ("undef", tuple(outs), a)
    80	        outs.append(m.out[(s, a)])
    81	        s = n
    82	    return ("ok", tuple(outs))
    83	
    84	
    85	def run(m, word):
    86	    return run_from(m, m.start, word)
    87	
    88	
    89	def _alphabet_union(m1, m2):
    90	    return tuple(sorted(set(m1.alphabet) | set(m2.alphabet), key=_sym_key))
    91	
    92	
    93	def shortest_bfs(m1, m2):
    94	    """Length of a shortest distinguishing word, or None if the machines agree on every word."""
    95	    if m1.kind != m2.kind:
    96	        return 0
    97	    alph = _alphabet_union(m1, m2)
    98	    if run(m1, ()) != run(m2, ()):
    99	        return 0
   100	    q = deque([(m1.start, m2.start, 0)])
   101	    seen = {(m1.start, m2.start)}
   102	    while q:
   103	        s1, s2, dist = q.popleft()
   104	        for a in alph:
   105	            wlen = dist + 1
   106	            r1 = run_from(m1, s1, (a,))
   107	            r2 = run_from(m2, s2, (a,))
   108	            if r1 != r2:
   109	                return wlen
   110	            n1 = m1.delta.get((s1, a))
   111	            n2 = m2.delta.get((s2, a))
   112	            if n1 is None or n2 is None:
   113	                continue
   114	            key = (n1, n2)
   115	            if key not in seen:
   116	                seen.add(key)
   117	                q.append((n1, n2, wlen))
   118	    return None
   119	
   120	
   121	def _ball(n_sym, length):
   122	    if length < 0:
   123	        return 0
   124	    total, power = 1, 1
   125	    for _ in range(length):
   126	        power *= max(n_sym, 1)
   127	        total += power
   128	        if total > 100000:
   129	            return total
   130	    return total
   131	
   132	
   133	def shortest_second(m1, m2):
   134	    """Second search. Brute force when the ball is small; otherwise an independent BFS."""
   135	    if m1.kind != m2.kind:
   136	        return 0
   137	    if run(m1, ()) != run(m2, ()):
   138	        return 0
   139	    alph = _alphabet_union(m1, m2)
   140	    # Independent BFS: index queue, symbols scanned in reverse order.
   141	    q = [(m1.start, m2.start)]
   142	    seen = {(m1.start, m2.start): 0}
   143	    head = 0
   144	    found = None
   145	    while head < len(q):
   146	        s1, s2 = q[head]
   147	        dist = seen[(s1, s2)]
   148	        head += 1
   149	        for a in reversed(alph):
   150	            r1 = run_from(m1, s1, (a,))
   151	            r2 = run_from(m2, s2, (a,))
   152	            if r1 != r2:
   153	                found = dist + 1
   154	                break
   155	            n1 = m1.delta.get((s1, a))
   156	            n2 = m2.delta.get((s2, a))
   157	            if n1 is None or n2 is None:
   158	                continue
   159	            key = (n1, n2)
   160	            if key not in seen:
   161	                seen[key] = dist + 1
   162	                q.append(key)
   163	        if found is not None:
   164	            break
   165	    if found is None:
   166	        return None
   167	    if _ball(len(alph), found) <= 100000:
   168	        brute = _shortest_brute(m1, m2, alph, found)
   169	        if brute != found:
   170	            raise RuntimeError(f"distinguishing lengths disagree: bfs {found} brute {brute}")
   171	        return brute
   172	    return found
   173	
   174	
   175	def _shortest_brute(m1, m2, alph, limit):
   176	    if run(m1, ()) != run(m2, ()):
   177	        return 0
   178	    words = [()]
   179	    for length in range(1, limit + 1):
   180	        nxt = []
   181	        for w in words:
   182	            if len(w) != length - 1:
   183	                continue
   184	            for a in alph:
   185	                nxt.append(w + (a,))
   186	        for w in nxt:
   187	            if run(m1, w) != run(m2, w):
   188	                return length
   189	        words.extend(nxt)
   190	    return None
   191	
   192	
   193	def _refine(blocks, alphabet, signature):
   194	    """Partition refinement. `signature(state, class_id)` returns a key."""
   195	    changed = True
   196	    while changed:
   197	        changed = False
   198	        nxt = []
   199	        index = {}
   200	        for i, block in enumerate(blocks):
   201	            for s in block:
   202	                index[s] = i
   203	        for block in blocks:
   204	            buckets = {}
   205	            for s in block:
   206	                key = signature(s, index)
   207	                buckets.setdefault(key, []).append(s)
   208	            if len(buckets) > 1:
   209	                changed = True
   210	            nxt.extend(buckets.values())
   211	        blocks = nxt
   212	    return blocks
   213	
   214	
   215	def minimize(m):
   216	    if m.kind == "moore":
   217	        buckets = {}
   218	        for s in m.states:
   219	            buckets.setdefault(m.out[s], []).append(s)
   220	        blocks = list(buckets.values())
   221	
   222	        def signature(s, index):
   223	            return tuple(index[m.delta[(s, a)]] for a in m.alphabet)
   224	
   225	    else:
   226	        buckets = {}
   227	        for s in m.states:
   228	            sig = tuple(m.out[(s, a)] for a in m.alphabet)
   229	            buckets.setdefault(sig, []).append(s)
   230	        blocks = list(buckets.values())
   231	
   232	        def signature(s, index):
   233	            return tuple(index[m.delta[(s, a)]] for a in m.alphabet)
   234	
   235	    blocks = _refine(blocks, m.alphabet, signature)
   236	    rep = {}
   237	    for block in blocks:
   238	        if m.kind == "moore":
   239	            r = min(block, key=lambda s: (str(m.out[s]), str(s)))
   240	        else:
   241	            r = min(block, key=str)
   242	        for s in block:
   243	            rep[s] = r
   244	    start = rep[m.start]
   245	    delta = {}
   246	    out = {}
   247	    have = set()
   248	    q = deque([start])
   249	    have.add(start)
   250	    while q:
   251	        s = q.popleft()
   252	        if m.kind == "moore":
   253	            out[s] = m.out[s]
   254	        for a in m.alphabet:
   255	            n = rep[m.delta[(s, a)]]
   256	            delta[(s, a)] = n
   257	            if m.kind == "mealy":
   258	                out[(s, a)] = m.out[(s, a)]
   259	            if n not in have:
   260	                have.add(n)
   261	                q.append(n)
   262	    if m.kind == "moore":
   263	        out.setdefault(start, m.out[start])
   264	    return Machine(m.kind, m.alphabet, start, delta, out)
   265	
   266	
   267	def canonical(m):
   268	    mm = minimize(m)
   269	    order = []
   270	    seen = {mm.start}
   271	    q = deque([mm.start])
   272	    while q:
   273	        s = q.popleft()
   274	        order.append(s)
   275	        for a in mm.alphabet:
   276	            n = mm.delta[(s, a)]
   277	            if n not in seen:
   278	                seen.add(n)
   279	                q.append(n)
   280	    idx = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(order)}
   281	    if mm.kind == "moore":
   282	        rows = tuple(
   283	            (idx[s], a, idx[mm.delta[(s, a)]], mm.out[mm.delta[(s, a)]])
   284	            for s in order
   285	            for a in mm.alphabet
   286	        )
   287	        return ("moore", mm.alphabet, mm.out[mm.start], rows)
   288	    rows = tuple(
   289	        (idx[s], a, idx[mm.delta[(s, a)]], mm.out[(s, a)])
   290	        for s in order
   291	        for a in mm.alphabet
   292	    )
   293	    return ("mealy", mm.alphabet, rows)
   294	
   295	
   296	def equivalent(m1, m2):
   297	    """(equal, length or None). Raises if the two methods disagree."""
   298	    same = canonical(m1) == canonical(m2)
   299	    length_a = shortest_bfs(m1, m2)
   300	    length_b = shortest_second(m1, m2)
   301	    if length_a != length_b:
   302	        raise RuntimeError(f"shortest word disagree {length_a} vs {length_b}")
   303	    if same != (length_a is None):
   304	        raise RuntimeError(f"canonical and product disagree: canonical_equal={same} length={length_a}")
   305	    return same, length_a
   306	
   307	
   308	def _sep_word(m, s1, s2):
   309	    if run_from(m, s1, ()) != run_from(m, s2, ()):
   310	        return ()
   311	    q = deque([(s1, s2, ())])
   312	    seen = {(s1, s2)}
   313	    while q:
   314	        a1, a2, word = q.popleft()
   315	        for a in m.alphabet:
   316	            r1 = run_from(m, a1, (a,))
   317	            r2 = run_from(m, a2, (a,))
   318	            nxt = word + (a,)
   319	            if r1 != r2:
   320	                return nxt
   321	            n1 = m.delta.get((a1, a))
   322	            n2 = m.delta.get((a2, a))
   323	            if n1 is None or n2 is None:
   324	                continue
   325	            key = (n1, n2)
   326	            if key not in seen:
   327	                seen.add(key)
   328	                q.append((n1, n2, nxt))
   329	    return None
   330	
   331	
   332	def characterise(m):
   333	    states = list(m.states)
   334	    acc = []
   335	    for i, s1 in enumerate(states):
   336	        for s2 in states[i + 1 :]:
   337	            if any(run_from(m, s1, w) != run_from(m, s2, w) for w in acc):
   338	                continue
   339	            w = _sep_word(m, s1, s2)
   340	            if w is None:
   341	                raise RuntimeError("minimised machine has indistinguishable states")
   342	            acc.append(w)
   343	    if not acc:
   344	        acc = [()]
   345	    return acc
   346	
   347	
   348	def transition_cover(m):
   349	    access = {m.start: ()}
   350	    q = deque([m.start])
   351	    while q:
   352	        s = q.popleft()
   353	        for a in m.alphabet:
   354	            n = m.delta[(s, a)]
   355	            if n not in access:
   356	                access[n] = access[s] + (a,)
   357	                q.append(n)
   358	    cover = set(access.values())
   359	    for word in list(access.values()):
   360	        for a in m.alphabet:
   361	            cover.add(word + (a,))
   362	    return list(cover)

   365	def _z_count(n_sym, k):
   366	    total, power = 1, 1
   367	    for _ in range(k):
   368	        power *= n_sym
   369	        total += power
   370	    return total

   373	def suite_cardinality(cover, chars, alphabet, k):
   374	    """Exact cardinality, or (None, upper) when the upper bound is above 5_000_000."""
   375	    zc = _z_count(len(alphabet), k)
   376	    upper = len(cover) * len(chars) * zc
   377	    if upper > 5_000_000:
   378	        return None, upper
   379	    base = len(alphabet) + 1
   380	    sid = {a: i + 1 for i, a in enumerate(alphabet)}
   381	
   382	    def pack(word):
   383	        x = 0
   384	        for sym in word:
   385	            x = x * base + sid[sym]
   386	        return x
   387	
   388	    words = set()
   389	    zs = [()]
   390	    for length in range(1, k + 1):
   391	        zs.extend(product(alphabet, repeat=length))
   392	    for p in cover:
   393	        for z in zs:
   394	            pz = p + z
   395	            for w in chars:
   396	                words.add(pack(pz + w))
   397	    return len(words), upper
   398	
   399	
   400	def _word_stream(p, w, alphabet, zlen):
   401	    if zlen == 0:
   402	        yield p + w
   403	        return
   404	    for z in product(alphabet, repeat=zlen):
   405	        yield p + z + w
   406	
   407	
   408	def shortest_suite_words(cover, chars, alphabet, k, limit):
   409	    """The first `limit` suite words in length-then-lex order.
   410	
   411	    Each (p, z, w) stream is already sorted, so a merge yields the same sequence as
   412	    sorting the whole set, without building a long length-class in memory.
   413	    """
   414	    chosen = []
   415	    seen = set()
   416	    max_len = max((len(p) for p in cover), default=0) + k + max((len(w) for w in chars), default=0)
   417	    for length in range(0, max_len + 1):
   418	        if len(chosen) >= limit:
   419	            break
   420	        streams = []
   421	        for p in cover:
   422	            for w in chars:
   423	                zlen = length - len(p) - len(w)
   424	                if 0 <= zlen <= k:
   425	                    streams.append(_word_stream(p, w, alphabet, zlen))
   426	        heap = []
   427	        for i, stream in enumerate(streams):
   428	            word = next(stream, None)
   429	            if word is None:
   430	                continue
   431	            heap.append((word_key(word), i, word))
   432	        heapq.heapify(heap)
   433	        while heap and len(chosen) < limit:
   434	            _, i, word = heapq.heappop(heap)
   435	            if word not in seen:
   436	                seen.add(word)
   437	                chosen.append(word)
   438	            nxt = next(streams[i], None)
   439	            if nxt is not None:
   440	                heapq.heappush(heap, (word_key(nxt), i, nxt))
   441	    return chosen
   442	
   443	
   444	def w_method(spec, other, m_bound, execute_cap=20000):
   445	    """Suite of `spec` under state bound `m_bound`, scored against `other`.
   446	
   447	    Returns size (exact or None), upper, whether it certifies, exposure at 16 and 64,
   448	    and how many words were executed.
   449	    """
   450	    spec_m = minimize(spec)
   451	    n = len(spec_m.states)
   452	    if m_bound < n:
   453	        return {
   454	            "certifies": False,
   455	            "reason": "m_below_n",
   456	            "n": n,
   457	            "m": m_bound,
   458	            "size": None,
   459	            "upper": None,
   460	            "exposed16": False,
   461	            "exposed64": False,
   462	            "executed": 0,
   463	        }
   464	    k = m_bound - n
   465	    cover = transition_cover(spec_m)
   466	    chars = characterise(spec_m)
   467	    size, upper = suite_cardinality(cover, chars, spec_m.alphabet, k)
   468	    words64 = shortest_suite_words(cover, chars, spec_m.alphabet, k, 64)
   469	    exposed16 = any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in words64[:16])
   470	    exposed64 = any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in words64)
   471	    executed = len(words64)
   472	    full_ok = True
   473	    if size is not None and size <= execute_cap:
   474	        # every word, not only the shortest 64
   475	        if size > len(words64):
   476	            all_words = shortest_suite_words(cover, chars, spec_m.alphabet, k, size)
   477	            executed = len(all_words)
   478	            if any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in all_words):
   479	                full_ok = False
   480	                exposed64 = True
   481	                if any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in all_words[:16]):
   482	                    exposed16 = True
   483	        elif any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in words64):
   484	            full_ok = False
   485	    elif size is None or size > execute_cap:
   486	        # k = 0 slice, plus the shortest 64 already run
   487	        k0 = []
   488	        for p in cover:
   489	            for w in chars:
   490	                k0.append(p + w)
   491	        k0 = list(set(k0))
   492	        executed += len(k0)
   493	        if any(run(spec_m, w) != run(other, w) for w in k0):
   494	            full_ok = False
   495	    return {
   496	        "certifies": True,
   497	        "reason": "ok",
   498	        "n": n,
   499	        "m": m_bound,
   500	        "k": k,
   501	        "size": size,
   502	        "upper": upper,
   503	        "exposed16": exposed16,
   504	        "exposed64": exposed64,
   505	        "executed": executed,
   506	        "full_agreement_on_executed": full_ok,
   507	        "truncated_execution": size is None or size > execute_cap,
   508	    }
   509	
   510	
   511	def reachable_states(m):
   512	    return len(m.states)

    72	def is_parse_error(err):
    73	    if not err or err == "too many calls":
    74	        return False
    75	    return any(m in err for m in PARSE_MARKERS)

    78	def label_of(tok, tid):
    79	    return tok.decode([tid]).strip()

    81	
    82	def program_text(head, tail, tok=None, call_tokens=None):
    83	    if "</program>" in head:
    84	        return head, "closed"
    85	    if len(head) < 80 or not tail:
    86	        return head, "trunc"
    87	    lo, hi = 80, len(head)
    88	    best = None
    89	    while lo <= hi:
    90	        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
    91	        suf = head[-mid:]
    92	        pos, start = [], 0
    93	        while True:
    94	            j = tail.find(suf, start)
    95	            if j < 0:
    96	                break
    97	            pos.append(j)
    98	            start = j + 1
    99	        if pos:
   100	            best = (mid, pos)
   101	            lo = mid + 1
   102	        else:
   103	            hi = mid - 1
   104	    if not best:
   105	        return head, "trunc"
   106	    mid, pos = best
   107	    conts = []
   108	    for j in pos:
   109	        rest = tail[j + mid :]
   110	        end = rest.find("</program>")
   111	        if end < 0:
   112	            return head, "trunc"
   113	        conts.append(rest[: end + len("</program>")])
   114	    if len(set(conts)) != 1:
   115	        return head, "ambiguous"
   116	    recovered = head + conts[0]
   117	    # The recorded call is the program plus the stop string "\\n<exec/>". A repeated row can
   118	    # satisfy the overlap rule across a gap, so a join that does not retokenise to the
   119	    # recorded call is not the program.
   120	    if tok is not None and call_tokens is not None:
   121	        n_prog = len(tok.encode(recovered).ids)
   122	        n_call = len(tok.encode(recovered + "\n<exec/>").ids)
   123	        if call_tokens not in (n_prog, n_call):
   124	            return head, "rejected"
   125	    return recovered, "stitched"
   126	
   127	
   128	def rows_of(tok, reg):
   129	    """(kind, start, delta, out or None, conflict)."""
   130	    if reg["kind"] == "moore":
   131	        delta, conflict = {}, False
   132	        for s, e, t in reg["rows"]:
   133	            key = (label_of(tok, s), label_of(tok, e))
   134	            nxt = label_of(tok, t)
   135	            if key in delta and delta[key] != nxt:
   136	                conflict = True
   137	            delta[key] = nxt
   138	        return {
   139	            "name": reg["name"],
   140	            "kind": "moore",
   141	            "start": label_of(tok, reg["start"]),
   142	            "delta": delta,
   143	            "out": None,
   144	            "conflict": conflict,
   145	        }
   146	    delta, out, conflict = {}, {}, False
   147	    for s, a, b, t, o in reg["rows"]:
   148	        key = (label_of(tok, s), (label_of(tok, a), label_of(tok, b)))
   149	        nxt, output = label_of(tok, t), label_of(tok, o)
   150	        if key in delta and (delta[key] != nxt or out[key] != output):
   151	            conflict = True
   152	        delta[key] = nxt
   153	        out[key] = output
   154	    return {
   155	        "name": reg["name"],
   156	        "kind": "mealy",
   157	        "start": label_of(tok, reg["start"]),
   158	        "delta": delta,
   159	        "out": out,
   160	        "conflict": conflict,
   161	    }
   162	
   163	
   164	def product_moore(regs):
   165	    alph = []
   166	    seen_a = set()
   167	    for reg in regs:
   168	        for (_, ev) in reg["delta"]:
   169	            if ev not in seen_a:
   170	                seen_a.add(ev)
   171	                alph.append(ev)
   172	    alph.sort()
   173	    start = tuple(reg["start"] for reg in regs)
   174	    delta = {}
   175	    q = deque([start])
   176	    seen = {start}
   177	    while q:
   178	        if len(seen) > 100000:
   179	            raise RuntimeError(f"product exceeded 100000 reachable states ({len(regs)} registers)")
   180	        state = q.popleft()
   181	        for ev in alph:
   182	            nxt = []
   183	            missing = False
   184	            for i, reg in enumerate(regs):
   185	                key = (state[i], ev)
   186	                if key not in reg["delta"]:
   187	                    # clock oracle: a register stays put on a symbol only another register names
   188	                    if any(ev == e for (_, e) in reg["delta"]):
   189	                        missing = True
   190	                        break
   191	                    nxt.append(state[i])
   192	                else:
   193	                    nxt.append(reg["delta"][key])
   194	            if missing:
   195	                continue
   196	            nxt_t = tuple(nxt)
   197	            delta[(state, ev)] = nxt_t
   198	            if nxt_t not in seen:
   199	                seen.add(nxt_t)
   200	                q.append(nxt_t)
   201	    return moore(alph, start, delta)
   202	
   203	
   204	def machine_from_regs(regs):
   205	    if not regs or any(r["conflict"] for r in regs):
   206	        return None
   207	    kinds = {r["kind"] for r in regs}
   208	    if kinds == {"moore"}:
   209	        return product_moore(regs)
   210	    if kinds == {"mealy"} and len(regs) == 1:
   211	        reg = regs[0]
   212	        alph = {sym for (_, sym) in reg["delta"]}
   213	        return mealy(alph, reg["start"], reg["delta"], reg["out"])
   214	    return None
   215	
   216	
   217	def ref_machine(ref):
   218	    if ref["mode"] == "tape":
   219	        regs = []
   220	        for table in ref["tables"]:
   221	            delta = {}
   222	            for s, e, n in table["rows"]:
   223	                delta[(str(s), str(e))] = str(n)
   224	            regs.append({"name": table["name"], "start": str(table["start"]), "delta": delta, "kind": "moore",
   225	                         "out": None, "conflict": False})
   226	        return product_moore(regs)
   227	    delta, out, alph = {}, {}, set()
   228	    for b, a, c, b2, d in RC.table_rows(ref["op"]):
   229	        sym = (str(a), str(c))
   230	        alph.add(sym)
   231	        delta[(str(b), sym)] = str(b2)
   232	        out[(str(b), sym)] = str(d)
   233	    return mealy(alph, "0", delta, out)
   234	
   235	
   236	def align(written, ref):
   237	    if ref["mode"] != "tape":
   238	        return written
   239	    ref_names = [t["name"] for t in ref["tables"]]
   240	    w_names = [r["name"] for r in written]
   241	    if len(w_names) == len(set(w_names)) and set(w_names) == set(ref_names):
   242	        by = {r["name"]: r for r in written}
   243	        return [by[n] for n in ref_names]
   244	    return written
   245	
   246	
   247	def partial_view(regs):
   248	    """One partial function for the prefix test. Moore product states are tuples."""
   249	    if not regs or any(r["conflict"] for r in regs):
   250	        return None
   251	    kinds = {r["kind"] for r in regs}
   252	    if kinds == {"mealy"} and len(regs) == 1:
   253	        reg = regs[0]
   254	        return "mealy", reg["start"], reg["delta"], reg["out"]
   255	    if kinds != {"moore"}:
   256	        return None
   257	    alph = sorted({ev for reg in regs for (_, ev) in reg["delta"]})
   258	    start = tuple(reg["start"] for reg in regs)
   259	    # known product transitions: a step is known when every register either has the row
   260	    # or does not use that symbol at all (clock self-loop). A missing row on a symbol the
   261	    # register does use is an unseen row, not a step.
   262	    delta, out = {}, {}
   263	    q = deque([start])
   264	    seen = {start}
   265	    out[start] = start
   266	    while q:
   267	        if len(seen) > 100000:
   268	            break
   269	        state = q.popleft()
   270	        for ev in alph:
   271	            nxt = []
   272	            known = True
   273	            for i, reg in enumerate(regs):
   274	                key = (state[i], ev)
   275	                if key in reg["delta"]:
   276	                    nxt.append(reg["delta"][key])
   277	                elif any(ev == e for (_, e) in reg["delta"]):
   278	                    known = False
   279	                    break
   280	                else:
   281	                    nxt.append(state[i])
   282	            if not known:
   283	                continue
   284	            nxt_t = tuple(nxt)
   285	            delta[(state, ev)] = nxt_t
   286	            out[nxt_t] = nxt_t
   287	            if nxt_t not in seen:
   288	                seen.add(nxt_t)
   289	                q.append(nxt_t)
   290	    return "moore", start, delta, out
   291	
   292	
   293	def refs_match(got, stored):
   294	    if got["answer"] != stored["answer"] or got["truth_values"] != stored["truth_values"]:
   295	        return False
   296	    if len(got["ref_calls"]) != len(stored["ref_calls"]):
   297	        return False
   298	    for a, b in zip(got["ref_calls"], stored["ref_calls"]):
   299	        if a["mode"] != b["mode"]:
   300	            return False
   301	        if a["mode"] == "tape":
   302	            if [t["name"] for t in a["tables"]] != [t["name"] for t in b["tables"]]:
   303	                return False
   304	            for ta, tb in zip(a["tables"], b["tables"]):
   305	                if ta["start"] != tb["start"]:
   306	                    return False
   307	                if {tuple(r) for r in ta["rows"]} != {tuple(r) for r in tb["rows"]}:
   308	                    return False
   309	            if list(a["tape"]) != list(b["tape"]):
   310	                return False
   311	        else:
   312	            if a["op"] != b["op"] or list(a["numbers"]) != list(b["numbers"]):
   313	                return False
   314	    return True
   315	
   316	
   317	def check_references(tok, stored_by_split):
   318	    rebuilt = {}
   319	    for tag, spec in CELLS.items():
   320	        cells = RL.parse_cells(spec, 16)
   321	        rebuilt[tag] = RL.make_problems(tok, cells, 777, 100)
   322	    r1 = rebuilt["r1a"] + rebuilt["r1b"]
   323	    r2 = rebuilt["r2"]
   324	    got = {"R1": r1, "R2": r2}
   325	    mismatches = []
   326	    for split, indexes in CHECK_AT.items():
   327	        for i in indexes:
   328	            if not refs_match(got[split][i], stored_by_split[split][i]):
   329	                mismatches.append((split, i, got[split][i]["cell"]))
   330	    return mismatches, {split: len(got[split]) for split in got}

   332	
   333	def anatomy_cause(record, problem):
   334	    if record["correct"]:
   335	        return None
   336	    template = record["cell"].split("|")[0]
   337	    calls = record["calls"]
   338	    first_err = next((c for c in calls if c.get("error") and c["error"] != "too many calls"), None)
   339	    if not calls:
   340	        return "no call" if record["finish"] == "stop" else "reading at length"
   341	    if first_err is not None:
   342	        return RA.classify_error(template, first_err["error"])
   343	    ran = [c for c in calls if not c.get("error")]
   344	    if any(c.get("reading_exact") is False for c in ran) and template not in RA.NOTES_TEMPLATES:
   345	        return "quote or notes mismatch"
   346	    if any(c.get("compile") is False for c in ran) and template not in RA.NOTES_TEMPLATES:
   347	        return "table (kind, alphabet or rows)"
   348	    if record["finish"] in ("length", "context"):
   349	        return "reading at length"
   350	    tv = problem.get("truth_values") or []
   351	    vals = [c.get("value") for c in ran]
   352	    if len(vals) >= len(tv) and all(v is None or a == v for a, v in zip(vals, tv)):
   353	        return "words after exact calls"
   354	    if template in RA.NOTES_TEMPLATES:
   355	        return "reading at length" if problem["L"] >= 1024 else "quote or notes mismatch"
   356	    return "other"
   357	
   358	
   359	def judged_pairs(calls, nref):
   360	    judged = []
   361	    k = 0
   362	    for call in calls:
   363	        err = call.get("error")
   364	        if err == "too many calls":
   365	            break
   366	        slot = k if k < nref else None
   367	        if err:
   368	            judged.append((slot, call))
   369	            break
   370	        judged.append((slot, call))
   371	        k += 1
   372	    return judged
   373	
   374	
   375	def parse_text(tok, text):
   376	    if not text.strip():
   377	        return {"error": "no program", "registers": [], "mode": None, "numbers": {}}
   378	    return RC.parse_call(tok, tok.encode(text).ids)
   379	
   380	
   381	def judge_call(tok, call, ref, ref_m, text, how):
   382	    err = call.get("error")
   383	    if is_parse_error(err):
   384	        return {"label": "unresolved", "why": "parse", "dist": None}
   385	    if call.get("mode") != ref["mode"]:
   386	        prog = parse_text(tok, text)
   387	        regs = align([rows_of(tok, r) for r in prog.get("registers") or []], ref) if prog.get("registers") else []
   388	        machine = machine_from_regs(regs) if regs and not prog.get("error") else None
   389	        if machine is not None and machine.total():
   390	            return {"label": "inequiv", "why": "mode", "dist": 0, "partial": False, "machine": machine, "ref": ref_m}
   391	        return {"label": "inequiv", "why": "mode", "dist": 0, "partial": True, "machine": None, "ref": ref_m, "view": None}
   392	    if call.get("compile") is True:
   393	        good = err is None and call.get("reading_exact") is True
   394	        return {
   395	            "label": "good" if good else "bad_input",
   396	            "why": "compile",
   397	            "dist": None,
   398	            "partial": False,
   399	            "machine": ref_m,
   400	            "ref": ref_m,
   401	        }
   402	    prog = parse_text(tok, text)
   403	    complete = "</program>" in text
   404	    if complete and prog.get("error"):
   405	        raise RuntimeError(f"clock accepted a program our parser rejected: {prog.get('error')}")
   406	    regs = align([rows_of(tok, r) for r in prog.get("registers") or []], ref) if prog.get("registers") else []
   407	    if complete:
   408	        machine = machine_from_regs(regs)
   409	        if machine is None or not machine.total():
   410	            raise RuntimeError("complete accepted program did not build a total machine")
   411	        same, dist = equivalent(machine, ref_m)
   412	        if not same:
   413	            return {"label": "inequiv", "why": "table", "dist": dist, "partial": False, "machine": machine, "ref": ref_m}
   414	        good = err is None and call.get("reading_exact") is True
   415	        return {
   416	            "label": "good" if good else "bad_input",
   417	            "why": "semantic",
   418	            "dist": None,
   419	            "partial": False,
   420	            "machine": machine,
   421	            "ref": ref_m,
   422	        }
   423	    view = partial_view(regs)
   424	    if view is not None:
   425	        kind, start, delta, out = view
   426	        word = prefix_disagrees(delta, out, start, kind, ref_m)
   427	        if word is not None:
   428	            if not replay_prefix(delta, out, start, kind, ref_m, word):
   429	                raise RuntimeError("prefix witness did not replay")
   430	            return {
   431	                "label": "unresolved",
   432	                "why": "prefix",
   433	                "dist": len(word),
   434	                "partial": True,
   435	                "how": how,
   436	            }
   437	    return {"label": "unresolved", "why": "truncated" if how != "closed" else "incomplete", "dist": None, "how": how}
   438	
   439	
   440	def classify_labels(labels, nref, judged_slots):
   441	    if any(x == "inequiv" for x in labels):
   442	        return "a"
   443	    judged_set = {s for s in judged_slots if s is not None}
   444	    missing = any(i not in judged_set for i in range(nref))
   445	    if any(x == "unresolved" for x in labels):
   446	        return "d"
   447	    if missing and all(x == "good" for x in labels):
   448	        return "d"
   449	    if any(x == "bad_input" for x in labels) and all(x in ("good", "bad_input") for x in labels):
   450	        return "b"
   451	    if labels and all(x == "good" for x in labels) and not missing:
   452	        return "c"
   453	    return "d"
   454	
   455	
   456	def suite_record(info):
   457	    return {
   458	        "certifies": bool(info.get("certifies")),
   459	        "reason": info.get("reason"),
   460	        "n": info.get("n"),
   461	        "m": info.get("m"),
   462	        "k": info.get("k"),
   463	        "size": info.get("size"),
   464	        "upper": info.get("upper"),
   465	        "exposed16": bool(info.get("exposed16")),
   466	        "exposed64": bool(info.get("exposed64")),
   467	        "truncated_execution": bool(info.get("truncated_execution")),
   468	    }
   469	
   470	
   471	def suites_for(spec, other, m_ref, m_written, cache):
   472	    out = {}
   473	    bounds = {"m_ref": m_ref, "m_written_plus": m_written}
   474	    for origin, machine in (("reference", spec), ("written", other)):
   475	        for bname, bound in bounds.items():
   476	            key = (origin, bname)
   477	            if bound is None or machine is None or spec is None or other is None:
   478	                out[f"{origin}|{bname}"] = {
   479	                    "certifies": False,
   480	                    "reason": "no_machine" if bound is not None else "no_bound",
   481	                    "size": None,
   482	                    "upper": None,
   483	                    "exposed16": False,
   484	                    "exposed64": False,
   485	                    "truncated_execution": False,
   486	                    "n": None,
   487	                    "m": bound,
   488	                    "k": None,
   489	                }
   490	                continue
   491	            ck = (id_of(machine), id_of(spec if origin == "written" else other), bound)
   492	            # cache by the two canonical forms and the bound, independent of origin direction
   493	            ckey = (canon_id(machine), canon_id(spec if origin == "written" else other), bound)
   494	            if ckey not in cache:
   495	                info = w_method(machine, spec if origin == "written" else other, bound)
   496	                if equivalent(machine, spec if origin == "written" else other)[0] and not info.get(
   497	                    "full_agreement_on_executed", True
   498	                ):
   499	                    raise RuntimeError(f"equivalent machines disagreed on the suite bound {bound}")
   500	                cache[ckey] = suite_record(info)
   501	            out[f"{origin}|{bname}"] = cache[ckey]
   502	            del key, ck
   503	    return out
   504	
   505	
   506	def id_of(machine):
   507	    return id(machine)
   508	
   509	
   510	def canon_id(machine):
   511	    from machines import canonical
   512	
   513	    return canonical(machine)
   514	
   515	
   516	def load_pairs():
   517	    grouped = {"R1": [], "R2": []}
   518	    for split, job, tag in JOBS:
   519	        records = json.loads((RES / job / f"{tag}_reason.json").read_text())["clock"]["records"]
   520	        problems = json.loads((RES / job / f"{tag}_problems.json").read_text())["problems"]
   521	        if len(records) != len(problems):
   522	            raise RuntimeError(f"{tag} record/problem length mismatch")
   523	        for i, (rec, prob) in enumerate(zip(records, problems)):
   524	            if rec["cell"] != prob["cell"] or rec["answer"] != prob["answer"][:200]:
   525	                raise RuntimeError(f"{tag} alignment failed at {i}")
   526	            grouped[split].append((rec, prob, tag, i))
   527	    return grouped

    25	def sha256_bytes(data):
    26	    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()

    29	def case_seed(case_id):
    30	    return int(hashlib.sha256(case_id.encode()).hexdigest()[:8], 16)

    33	def load_items(path, expect):
    34	    obj = json.loads(pathlib.Path(path).read_text())
    35	    got = obj.pop("sha256")
    36	    raw = json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(",", ":")).encode()
    37	    have = sha256_bytes(raw)
    38	    if have != got or have != expect:
    39	        raise SystemExit(f"items hash mismatch: file {have} embedded {got} argv {expect}")
    40	    obj["sha256"] = got
    41	    return obj

    57	            "oracle": oracle,
    58	            "user": user_text(body, case["rendered"], sentence),

    70	def done_ids(path):
    71	    if not path.exists():
    72	        return set()
    73	    ids = set()
    74	    for line in path.read_text().splitlines():
    75	        if line.strip():
    76	            ids.add(json.loads(line)["id"])
    77	    return ids

    80	def host_blob():
    81	    gpu = "none"
    82	    try:
    83	        gpu = subprocess.check_output(["nvidia-smi", "--query-gpu=name", "--format=csv,noheader"], text=True, timeout=5).strip()
    84	    except Exception:
    85	        pass
    86	    cpu = "unknown"
    87	    try:
    88	        info = pathlib.Path("/proc/cpuinfo").read_text()
    89	        bits = [line.split(":", 1)[1].strip() for line in info.splitlines()
    90	                if line.lower().startswith(("model name", "vendor_id", "cpu family", "model\t"))]
    91	        flags = next((line.split(":", 1)[1].split() for line in info.splitlines() if line.startswith("flags")), [])
    92	        cpu = " | ".join(bits[:4]) + f" | avx512f={'avx512f' in flags}"
    93	    except Exception:
    94	        pass
    95	    return {"gpu": gpu, "cpu": cpu}

    98	def merge_fresh(adapter, dest):
    99	    import gc
   100	    import shutil
   101	    import torch
   102	    from peft import PeftModel
   103	    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
   104	    if os.path.exists(dest):
   105	        shutil.rmtree(dest)
   106	    base = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(MODEL, dtype=torch.bfloat16)
   107	    merged = PeftModel.from_pretrained(base, adapter).merge_and_unload()
   108	    merged.save_pretrained(dest, safe_serialization=True)
   109	    AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL).save_pretrained(dest)
   110	    del base, merged
   111	    gc.collect()
   112	    if torch.cuda.is_available():
   113	        torch.cuda.empty_cache()

   116	def run_model(rows, model_path, mode, out_path, batch):
   117	    from vllm import LLM, SamplingParams, TokensPrompt
   118	    from transformers import AutoTokenizer
   119	    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_path)
   120	    im_end = tok.convert_tokens_to_ids("<|im_end|>")
   121	    llm = LLM(model=model_path, tokenizer=model_path, dtype="bfloat16", max_model_len=CONTEXT,
   122	              gpu_memory_utilization=0.85, seed=0, enable_prefix_caching=True)
   123	    pending = [r for r in rows if r["id"] not in done_ids(out_path)]
   124	    t0 = time.time()
   125	    for start in range(0, len(pending), batch):
   126	        chunk = pending[start:start + batch]
   127	        prompts, params, keep = [], [], []
   128	        for row in chunk:
   129	            text = THINK.format(q=row["user"]) if mode == "think" else CHAT.format(q=row["user"])
   130	            ids = tok.encode(text, add_special_tokens=False)
   131	            room = CONTEXT - len(ids) - 8
   132	            if room < 64:
   133	                write_jsonl(out_path, {"id": row["id"], "oracle": row["oracle"], "skipped": "context",
   134	                                       "prompt_tokens": len(ids)})
   135	                continue
   136	            cap = 4096 if mode == "think" else 512
   137	            if mode == "think":
   138	                sp = SamplingParams(temperature=0.6, top_p=0.95, top_k=20, max_tokens=min(cap, room),
   139	                                    seed=row["seed"], stop_token_ids=[im_end])
   140	            else:
   141	                sp = SamplingParams(temperature=0, max_tokens=min(cap, room), stop_token_ids=[im_end])
   142	            prompts.append(TokensPrompt(prompt_token_ids=ids))
   143	            params.append(sp)
   144	            keep.append(row)
   145	        if keep:
   146	            outs = llm.generate(prompts, params, use_tqdm=False)
   147	            for row, o in zip(keep, outs):
   148	                out = o.outputs[0]
   149	                write_jsonl(out_path, {
   150	                    "id": row["id"], "oracle": row["oracle"], "text": out.text,
   151	                    "tokens": len(out.token_ids), "finish": out.finish_reason,
   152	                    "prompt_tokens": len(o.prompt_token_ids),
   153	                })
   154	        print(json.dumps({"oracle": rows[0]["oracle"] if rows else mode, "done": min(start + batch, len(pending)),
   155	                          "of": len(pending), "seconds": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}), flush=True)
   156	    del llm
   157

   160	    dest = "/tmp/e4_merged"

   175	def main():
   176	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()

   178	    ap.add_argument("--expect-sha", required=True)
   179	    ap.add_argument("--split", choices=["smoke", "full"], required=True)
   180	    ap.add_argument("--stage", choices=["all", "loom", "base"], default="all")
   181	    ap.add_argument("--adapter", default="/reasonvol/reason-s1/adapter_step500")
   182	    ap.add_argument("--out", required=True)
   183	    a = ap.parse_args()

   187	        (out / "host.json").write_text(json.dumps(host_blob(), indent=1))

   192	            if r.returncode != 0:
   193	                raise SystemExit(r.returncode)
   194	        return

     1	"""Score E4 oracle outputs. Every number in the results section of E4.md is written here."""
     2
